prints both columns. **Controls**: C0a passes at both read-outs (every `bias_norms` entry exactly 0.0 across all forty replicates with `theta_drift` non-zero everywhere, min 0.0392 at 128), and the comparators are other epochs' artifacts admissible by the three per-replicate-identity precedents rather than by configuration identity — the fifth and sixth instance of that check. Cannot settle: **read-out 1307 is not measured here and is not claimed**, so the registration's third term is open; two read-outs with one comparator each, both at test 48; the mechanism stays an inference, since both read-outs show the body drifting MORE with the bias frozen while forgetting less and the locality itself is not measured; and **the residual at read-out 128 is 2.3σ from zero, so "the forgetting is gone" is nearly resolved and is not resolved** (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-bias-effect-is-not-a-read-out-32-artefact.md`) |
| `--methods naive,ewc --repeats 40 --fisher-batches 32 --lam 3e-3` at read-out 32 (`e133`) | C2b | **pre-registered before its run**: the frozen-bias arm removes 70% of the naive forgetting for free and is 0.20σ from the tuned diagonal penalty at the five seeds the paper quotes — so does the penalty beat the free constraint once BOTH are at forty replicates? (`e133`) | **done — BOTH PREDICTIONS FAIL, THE FALSIFIER FIRES IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION, AND THE PAPER'S DIAGONAL EWC HEADLINE DOES NOT SURVIVE ITS OWN CONFIGURATION AT EIGHT TIMES THE REPLICATES.** **C0a passes**: the naive arm is **per-replicate identical** to `runs/e116_r32_40reps.json` (first five +0.0417/+0.0521/+0.1042/+0.1146/+0.0521, as registered), so the comparison is paired on seeds. **The result at forty**: naive **+0.0750 ± 0.0088** against diagonal EWC **+0.0654 ± 0.0079**, a paired **−0.0096 ± 0.0080 = 1.21σ** — **P1 (≥ 3σ) FAILS** — and frozen-bias **+0.0227 ± 0.0033**, **−0.0427 ± 0.0086 = 4.98σ from EWC** — **P2 (< 2σ) FAILS**, and the falsifier's *direction* is inverted since it was *"EWC beats frozen-bias by ≥ 3σ"*. **At five replicates the diagonal removed 71% of the naive forgetting; at forty it removes 13%**, against a paper quoting the first at **2.47σ**, and its accuracy is *lower* than naive's at forty (0.8856 against 0.9125) where at five it was higher — **both metrics reverse**. **And it is not "EWC does nothing": it REDISTRIBUTES.** Per task, EWC reduces task 0's forgetting by **−0.0354 ± 0.0094 = **3.79σ**** and *increases* task 1's by **+0.0161 ± 0.0118**, so **the mean over the two is unresolved while both of its terms are moving, one of them decisively** — and with three tasks that mean IS the table's headline. **That is an eighth measurement trap**, distinct from *"resolves from zero is not differs from its neighbour"*: there a cell needed a comparator, here the comparator exists and two resolved components **cancel inside the aggregate**. **How favourable were the five seeds?** A **~1-in-55 draw**: their mean is +0.0208 against +0.0717 for replicates 6–40, their ranks among the forty are 10, 6, 9, 1, 16 — so **not** the five lowest — and **11,952 of the C(40,5) = 658,008 five-subsets are at least as favourable**, which is a draw five replicates cannot rule out. **What it changes**: §4.2's *"diagonal EWC finally resolves"* caption and table, the abstract's *"EWC helps only when the read-out is narrow enough"* (now doubly qualified, since at forty it does not resolve there either and a free constraint beats it by 4.98σ), and the trap list, which goes to eight. Cannot settle: **one read-out, one task order, one circuit, and the task order matters more than usual because the mechanism is a trade between task 0 and task 1** — `mean_forgetting` over two numbers is exactly the statistic shown to be the wrong summary; it does not say EWC is useless and does not re-open the theory, since LGCL's 33%-lossy claim is about the linear substrate and untouched; **it does not bound what a different λ would do**, since the configuration is the paper's own hardened one and a λ sweep at forty replicates is the natural follow-up; and **the block and replay rows are not re-measured here**, so the finding is about the diagonal row alone until `e135` lands (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-diagonal-ewc-does-not-survive-its-own-configuration.md`) |
| `e137_bias_substitution.py` over the forty-replicate naive and ewc arms (`e137`) | C2b | **analysis, no runs**: `e125` says the channel carries 70% of the forgetting and `e133` says the penalty is 1.21σ from naive — two facts about the same channel, so **where does the adaptation go when the penalty constrains `theta`?** (`e137`) | **done — THE PENALTY DOES NOT REMOVE THE ADAPTATION, IT RELOCATES IT INTO THE CHANNEL IT CANNOT SEE.** At the same forty paired seeds: EWC's `theta_drift` is **0.0357 against naive's 0.0498** (ratio 1.395, i.e. **28% less**, **+0.0141 ± 0.0006 = 25.24σ**) while its bias movement is **2.5027 against 2.0005** (ratio 0.799, i.e. **25% MORE**, **−0.5022 ± 0.0530 = 9.48σ**). **So the code claim — `model.bias` appears in `e8_rate_network.py` only in the optimiser's parameter list, the drift record and the checkpoint save, and `clfly/network/fisher.py` does not mention it — has a measured consequence stronger than "unpenalised": the channel is not merely ignored, it ABSORBS what the penalty removes from the weights it does touch.** **And that is the mechanism behind `e133`'s null, which is more informative than "the five-replicate effect did not replicate":** constraining `theta` by 28% buys nothing on the forgetting (1.21σ) **because the adaptation moved to the channel carrying 70% of it** — the penalty squeezes one end and the unpenalised end grows. It also makes §8's bias-penalty arm **a different method rather than a better λ**: such a penalty would act on **100%** of the body instead of 97.1%, which is the only version of the experiment in which the penalty can act on the whole thing. **And section 2 is the first trajectory quantity whose SIGN lines up with the channel that carries the forgetting**: across the forty seeds of one arm, `bias_from_zero` gives **r = +0.340 (Spearman +0.327, t = +2.23)** against the forgetting, the largest of four quantities tried (bias's last step +0.263, `theta_drift` **−0.102**, fit depth +0.048) — where `e108`, `e109` and `e121` found the drift, the interference terms and the fit depth unable to order it at all. **It is a LEAD and not a result**, for three reasons printed beside it: the same quantity is **−0.101** in the EWC arm; the **per-task** steps are all ≈ 0 (r from −0.042 to +0.101 over four task-arm combinations), so the correlation is with the **cumulative** movement and not the task-specific one; and fifteen quantities have now been correlated with the forgetting here, so one at ~3% is close to what multiplicity alone would give. **And it is not in tension with `e125`**: the channel's own seed-to-seed variation explains **0.340² = 12%** of the forgetting's variation while its LEVEL carries 70% of the effect — a quantity can matter enormously and still be nearly constant between the runs being compared, since the whole channel varies by 8% of its own magnitude across seeds. **A large intervention effect and a weak correlation are evidence about two different things.** Cannot settle: **one read-out, one λ, one task order, one circuit** — and whether a larger λ relocates MORE is the natural prediction and therefore the natural test, which a λ sweep at forty replicates can now look for as a mechanism rather than a null; the substitution is a between-arm comparison of forty paired seeds, so it is resolved but says nothing about *how* the optimiser chooses; the lead is one arm, four quantities and forty seeds, with the sign absent in the other arm and needing its own pre-registration to become anything else; and nothing here says the bias's movement is *harmful*, since `e125`'s statement is about the level and a seed whose bias moves more may still forget less — the correlation's weak sign says it does not (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-penalty-relocates-the-adaptation.md`) |
| `e135_r32_methods_{plastic,frozenbias}.json` — the five-method table, both arms (`e135`) | C2b | **pre-registered before its runs**: `e125` measured that 70% of the naive forgetting lives where no penalty looks, so **does the comparison BETWEEN the five methods survive its removal from every arm?** The prediction was sharp because the mechanism is checkable: `model.bias` appears in `e8_rate_network.py` only in the optimiser's parameter list, the drift record and the checkpoint save and `fisher.py` never mentions it, so the bias's contribution should be **method-independent** — every contrast unchanged, every level down (`e135`) | **done — the three Fisher penalties are exactly as the code predicts and REPLAY IS NOT, and it is the paper's strongest claim that moves.** **C0a passed on three controls at once**: the plastic arm reproduces `e8_hardened_basis`'s **naive** and **ewc** rows and `e61_replay96_step8`'s **replay** row **per replicate** — and the `ewc` match is the strongest of the three, because its numbers pass through the Fisher's own arithmetic and the anchor, so the *penalty* is epoch-stable and not merely the plastic arm. **P1 fails and P2 fails, exclusively on the replay arm, and the falsifier does not fire**: the three penalties' contrasts against naive move by **0.42σ** (ewc), **0.21σ** (block) and **0.48σ** (block-rand) — **method-independent, exactly as the code says** — while **replay's advantage is CUT BY 66%, from −0.0854 to −0.0292**, a move of **+0.0563 ± 0.0252 = 2.23σ**, and replay is the only level that **rises** (−0.0125 → −0.0042). The falsifier was registered at **≥ 3σ**, so **the registration stated the prediction, named replay as the likeliest mover without predicting which** (*"its arm has something the others do not"*), and **the risk materialised at a size the registration had already bounded** — which is the registration behaving as a design. **The mechanism is the arithmetic of a baseline**: replay's own forgetting is already **below zero** in both arms, so **there is no bias-channel forgetting left in it to remove**, while naive had **0.0479** of its forgetting in that channel — so freezing the bias helps the baseline a great deal and replay not at all, and their comparison was partly a comparison between a method that forgets and **a baseline that forgets through a channel the method does not use**. **Which makes the paper's strongest network claim its most confound-sensitive one**: §4.2's replay row is **−0.0854 ± 0.0129 = 6.6σ**, the number the project had to *recreate*; with the channel removed from every arm it is **−0.0292** — **a method that drives the forgetting to zero cannot be credited with the forgetting a baseline does not have to suffer either**. **And the accuracies agree**: replay's final accuracy falls **0.9681 → 0.9472** while the three penalty arms' mostly *rise* (naive 0.9139 → 0.9333, block 0.9153 → 0.9319, block-rand 0.9278 → 0.9333, ewc 0.9222 → 0.9181) — **replay is the one method that gets worse on both metrics when the confound is removed from all five arms**. **What it changes in the paper**: with the bias frozen in every arm the ranking by forgetting is **ewc (−0.0104) < replay (−0.0042) < block-rand (+0.0104) < block (+0.0167) < naive (+0.0250)**, so **diagonal EWC is now the best method and replay second**, where the plastic table has replay first by a wide margin — so *"replay is the stronger method in every setting"* is a claim about the **plastic** configuration and is partly a claim about the baseline's unpenalised channel. **What it does not change**: the penalties' mutual ordering (ewc best, block worse, matched-random between) is **identical in both arms**, which is the paper's central network result — the *basis* question — with the confound removed. **The three Fisher arms are confound-robust; the replay comparison is not.** And it does not say replay is bad: its forgetting is ≤ 0 in both arms, which no penalty achieves; what it says is that its margin over naive is 66% smaller when the thing naive forgets through is taken away. Cannot settle: **five replicates**, matching the paper's table, so 2.23σ is a suggestion and the 0.2σ figures are a bound five points can carry for a small effect only — **the falsifier's threshold is why this is not a refutation**, and the honest form is that the contrast moved by more than the registration allowed and less than its falsifier; one configuration (read-out 32, three tasks), which is where the confound is **largest** (70%, against 89% and 83% at read-out 128 and 1307 where the absolute amounts are smaller); the within-arm comparisons are untouched by the baseline arithmetic and all stand as they were; and the two arms are the same seeds but the plastic side's control is `e135`'s own arm, which matched three other-epoch artifacts per replicate (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-confound-removal-changes-one-contrast.md`) |
| `e139_whole_body_interference.py` over `e133`'s forty paired seeds, plus the `whole_body` block added to the runner (`e139`) | C2b | **analysis + instrument**: `e125` says the offsets carry 70% of the forgetting, `e137` that the penalty relocates adaptation into them, `e133` that the penalty's forgetting moves 1.21σ — **so does the penalty reduce the interference it was designed to reduce?** (`e139`) | **done — IT DOES, BY A FACTOR OF FORTY-FIVE, AND NOTHING HAPPENS: the interference instrument reads one channel and the effect lives in the other.** At forty paired seeds, on task 0: the `theta`-only first-order term falls **+0.2336 → +0.0052**, a **98% reduction** (ratio **0.022**, **−0.2284 ± 0.0221 = **10.34σ****), the cosine falls **0.0476 → 0.0019** (ratio 0.040, **14.41σ**) and the displacement **18.817 → 14.142** (ratio 0.752, **28.25σ**); on task 1 the same terms fall at **9.04σ** and **10.41σ**. **And the forgetting, on the same artifact and the same seeds, moves 1.21σ.** So **diagonal EWC does exactly what its theory says — and the effect it exists to predict is unchanged**, which is a **manipulation** and not a correlation: `e108` found the term could not *order* the forgetting across configurations, and this moves it 45-fold with an intervention and the effect does not move. **A quantity that can be moved 45-fold without the effect moving is not the effect's carrier, and no correlation can show that as cleanly as an intervention can.** **The explanation is that `interference` is built from `_grad_j(θ)` and `θ_final − θ_j` — `theta` ALONE** — while `e125` measured that **70%** of the forgetting is carried by the 800 offsets and `e137` that the penalty's effect on `theta` is matched by an **opposite movement** in them; **so the one-line account is evaluated over the half the penalty squeezed and not the half the damage moved to, and a term falling 45-fold in a channel holding 30% of the effect can leave the effect untouched.** **Which re-reads `e108`'s null**: the term failed to order the forgetting and the reason is now visible — **it was measuring the wrong half**. **And task 1 carries a detail that supports the redistribution reading**: its *gradient* norm **rises** under the penalty (0.2452 → 0.3608, ratio **1.471**, **3.73σ**), which is what a body that has moved *away* from task 1's optimum looks like, and is the same statement as `e133`'s per-task finding that EWC's task-1 forgetting is *worse* than naive's. The second-order term does **not** differ between the arms (1.29σ and 1.37σ), so what is established is that the **linear** account falls 45-fold. **The instrument was extended in the same fire**: `whole_body_grad` returns the gradient with respect to both halves, the `interference` block stores `whole_body.{per_task,cumulative,theta_only_cumulative,bias_only_cumulative,bias_grad_norm,bias_displacement_norm}`, and a test asserts the decomposition's arithmetic on a real run (the whole is the sum of its halves). **And the follow-up is registered here rather than after the fact** (`e139`'s run, naive and ewc at forty paired seeds on this configuration): **P1** the whole-body first-order term is **NOT** reduced as the `theta`-only one is, ratio **above 0.5** against 0.022, which follows from `e137`'s measured substitution since the bias's displacement *grows* 25% under the penalty; **P2** the bias's own contribution is a **substantial share** of the whole-body term in the naive arm; **falsifier** the whole-body ratio is **below 0.1**, i.e. the body-wide term falls about as hard as the `theta`-only one — then channel-blindness is **not** the explanation and the forgetting's insensitivity must be explained by something other than where the instrument was pointed. **AND IT IS NOW MEASURED, AND P1 HOLDS WITH THE FALSIFIER SILENT**: on the same forty paired seeds and task 0, the whole-body term is **+0.4266 → +0.3257**, ratio **0.763** at **1.39σ**, against the θ-only form's 0.022 at 10.34σ — and **the bias's own contribution is 45%** of the naive arm's whole-body term (0.1930 against 0.2336), which is P2, while **under the penalty that contribution *grows* 66%** (+0.1274 ± 0.0701 = 1.82σ, 0.1930 → 0.3204). **So the θ-only instrument was reading 55% of the account, and its share of the whole-body term collapses from 98% to 2% between the arms — the penalty does not leave the instrument pointed at one channel, it empties that channel and fills the other.** **And the three-arm extension is the strongest form of the result**: `e138`'s anchored arm carries the same instrument at the same seeds, and the whole-body ordering **0.4266 → 0.3257 → 0.1831** (the last at ratio **0.429, 4.21σ** from naive, against the unanchored arm's **1.39σ**) is **exactly the forgetting's ordering** 0.0750 → 0.0654 → 0.0292, while the θ-only form's **0.2336 → 0.0052 → 0.0238** is **lowest in the arm whose forgetting is highest of the three penalties**. **The extended instrument tracks the effect and the old one does not** — so the interference account is not discarded but **repaired**, by measuring the body the penalty acts on rather than the half the original formulation named. **And a lead sits beside it, the same size as the last one**: within the naive arm the `theta`-only first-order term correlates with the forgetting at **r = +0.329** and **−0.221** in the EWC arm — the **second** quantity in this session at about +0.33 whose sign does not survive a change of arm, the first being the bias's movement (+0.340 / −0.101) — and **two at 3% in the same arm is what multiplicity produces**, so that is a reason to be more suspicious of both rather than less. Cannot settle: **the whole-body measurement is task 0 and three arms** (`e139`'s naive and EWC plus `e138`'s strong anchor), so the ordering is three points and **task 1's whole-body term is not reported**; the first-order term is a **linearisation** and the second-order one does *not* differ between the arms, so what is established is that the linear account falls 45-fold in one channel and 24% over the body and not that a nonlinear one would move; one read-out, one λ, three tasks, one circuit, and it is the configuration with the **largest** channel share (70%, against 89% and 83% at read-outs 128 and 1307); and nothing here says the penalty is mis-specified — it says the penalty acts on one channel and the forgetting lives in the other, which is what makes `e138`'s bias-anchored arm the only version in which the penalty can act on the whole body (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-interference-instrument-is-channel-blind.md`) |
| `--readout-size {128,700} --methods naive,replay --repeats 40`, its two artifacts to be written as `e155_r{128,700}_naive_replay.json` under `runs/` (`e155`) | C2b | **pre-registered before its runs and LAUNCHED on the free slot.** `e148`'s finding proposed that **`replay`'s margin over `naive` is simply the baseline's forgetting** (replay sits at ≈ 0, so the margin is whatever the baseline had), and it fits four configurations with no free parameter — base 1.045, shared-input 0.921, and the two frozen arms 1.17 (5 reps) and **0.918 (40 reps, the model's one out-of-sample test, and it passed)**. **This run varies a different thing: the read-out**, where the baseline's forgetting is already measured at five levels (`e116`, forty replicates each): **+0.0750 at 32, +0.0370 at 128, +0.0221 at 700, +0.0219 at 900, +0.0357 at 1307**. **P1**: each arm's margin lies within **2s** of its own baseline's forgetting — **-0.0370 +/- 0.0051 at 128** and **-0.0221 +/- 0.0047 at 700** (the sems are `e116`'s and they are the right ones because both quantities share the forty seeds; a paired sem of ~0.004 puts the window at about +/-0.008). **Falsifier**: either margin is more than 2s away — in particular **the channel-share reading `e135` motivated**, which at 128 predicts **-0.89 x 0.0370 = -0.0329** if the offsets carry the 89% `e134` measured there. **And what this design cannot separate, stated before the run as rule 41 requires**: at 128 the two predictions are **0.0041 apart**, inside each other's 2s windows, so **that arm tests generalisation and not discrimination**; at 700 the share is unmeasured, and at a share near 60% the channel reading predicts -0.0133 against the model's -0.0221, **0.0088 apart ≈ 2.2s**, so **the 700 arm is the discriminating one**. **C0**: both new `naive` rows must be per-replicate identical to `e116`'s at the same read-outs — a reproduction test on two configurations, per-replicate and never an aggregate. **C1 (instrument)**: replay's own forgetting must be near zero, or the model's premise does not hold at these read-outs | **BOTH ARMS HAVE LANDED AND THE DISCRIMINATING ONE DID DISCRIMINATE, AGAINST THE CHANNEL READING.** **C0 passes exactly in both**: each run's `naive` row is **per-replicate identical to `e116`'s** at the same read-out (forgetting and accuracy, worst difference 0) — **the third and fourth *arm* repeats of this session, and the correction matters**: these two runs' configs differ from `e116`'s in `lam` (1.0 against 0.003) and `fisher_batches` (32 against 8), **neither of which the `naive` arm reads**, so the identity is rule 39's benign twin — **what has been executed twice is the arm, not the configuration** (rule 44). Read-out 128: `naive` **+0.0370**, `replay` **-0.0096**, margin **-0.0466 +/- 0.0058 = 8.07s**, which is **1.25s** from the floor model's -0.0370 and 1.78s from the channel reading's -0.0329 — the arm the registration said **could not** discriminate, and it did not. **Read-out 700 is the one that could**: `naive` **+0.0221** (matching `e116`), `replay` **+0.0008**, margin **-0.0214 +/- 0.0045 = 4.71s**, which is the floor model's -0.0221 to **+0.0008 (0.12s)** — and **for the channel reading to survive there the offsets would have to carry `0.0214/0.0221 = 97%` of that baseline's forgetting against the `89%` and `83%` measured at the neighbouring read-outs**, so the proportional reading is refuted by the share's own trend. **And the identity holds in all five configurations**: `1 - replay_own/baseline` reads 1.045, 0.922, 0.921, 1.259, 0.964 against the measured ratios 1.045, 0.921, 0.916, 1.259, 0.968, with **`replay`'s own forgetting pooled at -0.0004 +/- 0.0030 over the five** (0.14s from zero, a 2s bound of **+/-0.0059**). The registrations are `docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-floor-model-across-the-readout-axis-registered.md`; the readings are `docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-floor-models-residual-is-replays-own-forgetting.md`, `docs/findings/2026-09-24-replays-own-forgetting-pooled.md` and `docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-discriminating-arm-refutes-the-channel-reading.md` | 
| `e154_retention_matrix_audit.py` over every artifact that carries a retention matrix (129 artifacts, 243 method-arms) (`e154`) | C2b | **an audit, no runs, and it asks whether two of the day's claims are structural rather than empirical**: `e151` and `e152` recommend the newest task's accuracy as a column and call the aggregate one-sided, both argued from measurements. **If the complements are structural, no re-weighting of the aggregate can recover the column** and the recommendation is not a preference (`e154`) | **done — THE AGGREGATE AND THE NEWEST TASK'S ACCURACY ARE COMPLEMENTS BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THE ACCURACY METRIC IS A LOWER BOUND ON THE LITERATURE'S.** **Three identities hold to the last digit over 243 method-arms (worst difference 0)**: `learned[j] == R[j, j]`; `learned[T-1] == final_per_task[T-1]`; and **`forgetting_per_task[j] == learned[j] - final_per_task[j]`** — the third because the runner fills `R` for `j <= k` only, so its window `nanmax(R[:j+1, j])`, which *looks* like the standard "best accuracy the task ever had", **contains exactly one finite entry: the diagonal**. **So what the project calls forgetting is acquisition-minus-final**, which makes `mean_forgetting` **pure retention**, the newest task's accuracy **pure acquisition** (nothing is trained after it, so no interference can be in it), and `final_accuracy` **the only mixture** — **the column `e151`/`e152` recommend and the aggregate it is recommended beside are complements for a structural reason, and no re-weighting of the mean recovers the column**. **And the convention costs something**: the literature's accuracy forgetting takes the max over the **later** checkpoints, which can only be larger, and over **5,762** (task, replicate) observations that gap is **strictly positive in 15.2%** of them, mean **0.00449**, p90 **0.02083**, max **0.10417** — so **every forgetting number in the paper is a lower bound on the statistic a reader from that literature would compute**, while the contrasts inside it are unaffected because both sides use the same form. **And the loss-valued metric uses the OTHER window** (`L[T-1, j] - nanmin(L[j:, j])`), which is **load-bearing rather than decorative**: `L[j, j] - nanmin(L[j:, j])` is off-diagonal in **5.8%** of 3,060 observations (mean 0.000234, max 0.00651) — so of the project's two metrics, **the accuracy one is the diagonal form and the loss one is already the conventional form**, a convention mismatch between two quantities the paper quotes side by side (`e123` had measured the loss window to be inert on one configuration; over the corpus it is not). Cannot settle: the identities are structural but the **15.2% and 5.8% are measurements of this benchmark's dynamics** (one read-out, three tasks, cs 800); the observations are **not independent** (129 artifacts, 243 arms, shared seeds) and the gap distribution is zero-inflated (median 0), so the share and the percentiles are the summary rather than the mean; and it says nothing about *which* tasks peak later (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-aggregate-and-the-last-task-are-complements-by-construction.md`) |
| `e158_correction_index.py` over the findings corpus and the paper — the self-corrections this project has marked, and where each says it lands (`e158`) | C2b | **an index, no runs, and it closes the one audit class this project had none of**: tables (`e105`), enumerations (`e126`), derivations (`e132`) and the programme table (`e127`) are all checked, and **none of them covers corrections** — so the record's true state is spread between the paper, the plan and the findings, and a reader arriving fresh cannot tell from the paper alone which of its sentences has since been qualified (`e158`) | **done — 45 MARKED SELF-CORRECTIONS IN 39 FINDINGS, AND ONLY SIX OF THEM SAY WHERE THEY LAND; THE PAPER CARRIES 8 MARKERS UNDER THE SAME CONVENTIONS.** The index finds every sentence carrying one of **nine explicit conventions** (`CORRECTED`, `corrected in place`, `must be corrected`, `is withdrawn`, `was wrong`, `is REFUTED`, `was REFUTED`, `this sentence used to say`, `read here until 2026-`) and attributes a `§N` to the paper **only when the sentence says "paper"** — without that rule it attributed nine corrections to the paper and **six of them were findings talking to themselves**, which is the attribution defect the test pins. **It is deliberately an index and not a checker**: matching a correction to the place it was applied needs a judgement about whether two sentences say the same thing, and a scanner that guesses is worse than none (rule 22) — what it does exactly is find the sentences and **count the unnamed ones, 39 of 45, which are the corrections no reader will ever find by searching the paper**. nothing once the channel is held still*) is refuted at **5.53s**, **and the falsifier's consequence clause falls with it** — the falsifier said λ's whole effect runs through the uncovered channel, other axis: **newest-task accuracy 0.9073 against 0.8750, +0.0323 = 5.74s**, mean accuracy **+0.0168 = 6.08s** — **the reverse of the free channel, where it is the stability axis that moves (4.27s THE FLOOR IS WHERE THE EFFECT IS: A WEAKER PENALTY WINS ON BOTH METRICS.** **λ = 3e-4, five times below every point any sweep here has tried, resolves against `naive` at −0.0354 ± 0.0083 = 4.27σ** over forty paired seeds — 27/40 negative on each task — **with both forgettable tasks improved** (task 0 −0.0422 at 3.54σ, task 1 −0.0286 at 2.87σ, where λ = 3e-3 at forty has its second task *worse*) **and accuracy not worse than `naive`'s** (+0.0049 ± 0.0053 = 0.91σ, against the 2.69σ that λ = 3e-3 costs). It is also **3.74σ better than λ = 3e-3 on forgetting with 6.00σ better accuracy**, and **4.27σ** is **1.21σ** from the anchored arm's 4.84σ, i.e. indistinguishable — so *"weaken the penalty"* and *"cover the channel"* are two routes to a level this artifact cannot separate. **So "λ = 0.003 is the only useful setting" was a statement about a grid whose floor was the optimum it found**, and the paper's §4.7 sentence *"at λ = 0.003 all three Fisher variants do beat naive"* was right about the setting and wrong about the resolution — the same shape as §4.2's diagonal row. **And the relocation is present even at the weak end**: the bias's cumulative path is **4.21σ above `naive`'s**, so weakening λ reduces the forgetting without removing the substitution. **A lead this fire also killed**: ordered by that path the three penalty arms looked perfectly monotone (3.4452 → 0.0292, 3.6835 → 0.0396, 4.2819 → 0.0654), and **`e138`'s two arms break it at four points** — sorted by forgetting the paths run up, down, up — so the relation holds **within each knob and not across two**, and accuracy, mean θ drift and the bias's final distance from zero are equally non-monotone, i.e. **the four penalty arms are not a one-parameter family** (rule 36 now carries this). **The registration's P1 and P2b HOLD in the strongest form the statistics allow and P2 FAILS, with the falsifier silent — the sweep is complete** and its four verdicts are: P1 the bias's path rises monotonically in λ — **3.6835 → 4.2819 → 5.4367 → 6.2313 across 3e-4, 3e-3, 3e-2, 3e-1, with the registered top contrast at +1.9494 ± 0.1173 = 16.63σ and 0 of 40 replicates against it**; P2 *"the forgetting does not fall as λ rises"*, registered as λ = 3e-2 worse than 3e-3 at ≥2σ, **measures +0.0156 ± 0.0098 = 1.60σ — the right direction and 0.4σ below the bar, the second registered threshold today to land inside its own uncertainty, **and the FIRST case in which the metric itself decided the verdict: the same forty pairs in the same artifact give +0.0651 ± 0.0154 = 4.24σ on the loss-valued forgetting the runner was already recording, and the two metrics rank those seeds at +0.51 to +0.71 within each of five arms — so the registered form fails while the question it asked is answered *yes* by another quantity in the same file, which is why rule 37 now requires a registration to name the metric as well as the form (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-metric-decides-the-threshold.md`)**; P2b θ drift falls while the forgetting does not — **−0.0095 ± 0.0004 = 22.35σ with 40 of 40 replicates below**; and the falsifier does not fire — the top arm is 1.74σ *worse* than 3e-3 rather than ≥3σ better. **And the sweep's shape is a BRACKETED interior optimum**: the forgetting reads **+0.0750 at λ = 0, +0.0396 at 3e-4, +0.0654 at 3e-3, +0.0810 at 3e-2 and +0.0846 at 3e-1**, so it falls from 0 to 3e-4 and rises afterwards — **λ* ∈ (0, 3e-3) with 3e-4 the best point measured, and the bracket two decades wide, so the licensed sentence is "the best point measured" (rule 38) rather than "the optimum"**; the top two arms are **dominated on both metrics** (3e-2 and 3e-1 are no better than `naive` on forgetting, at 0.58σ and 0.85σ, with accuracy 8.33σ and 8.86σ below it), and the dose–response is a **four-point** relation rather than the three-point lead that died earlier in the same fire; **and the single-lambda optimum no longer needs rule 38's caveat about an unbracketed edge, because the bracket now has the unpenalised arm as its lower end** — what remains unbracketed is only the *location* inside it. Cannot settle: one read-out, one circuit, three tasks, **one Fisher batch count (32) — so λ and the batch count are confounded as everywhere in this project**; the optimum is **bracketed rather than located** — two decades wide, with the unpenalised arm as its lower end — so the licensed sentence is *the best point measured*, and `e101`'s sweep found the batch count moves the penalty's effective strength; and the best λ found is 1.21σ from the best anchored arm, so a *weaker and anchored* arm is what would separate coverage from strength (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-lambda-sweeps-floor-is-where-the-effect-is.md`) |

| `runs/e58_bases_18seeds_perseed.json` cross-read against `runs/e66_named_bases_seed_robustness.json`, prompted by `e105`'s repaired check | C2b | **analysis, no runs**: the sharp check stopped counting §4.4's `delta vs the diagonal` column as a failure and left it **unchecked**, which leaves the question the column cannot answer for itself — **does the paragraph's reference value close against the table's deltas?** | **done — NO: THE WORST OF FOURTEEN WAS A DIFFERENT CANDIDATE.** §4.4's last paragraph says the adaptive truncation *"is the worst of all fourteen (+0.0222 against the diagonal's +0.01762)"*, and the same eighteen-seed run's artifact holds all fifteen keys: sorted by excess at **d = 1307** the top three are **`rank4` 0.0223347**, `rank16` 0.0223113 and **`rank64` 0.0222016**, with the diagonal at 0.0176167. **So the worst of the fourteen is `rank4` at +0.0223, and the quoted +0.0222 is `rank64`'s** — a real number from the same run, which is exactly why no reader could catch it. **The identity that makes it checkable is subtraction between two artifacts**: `e58` stores each basis's absolute excess and `e66` stores each basis's paired delta against the diagonal, and `0.022334689567 − 0.017616731227 = 0.004717958340` matches `e66`'s stored delta **to floating-point precision** — so the artifacts agree, the table is right, and the prose is the only place the error can be; the paragraph's other two numbers are **correct** (+0.01762 is 0.0176167, +0.01270 is 0.0127022, and the 28% is 27.9%). **And it is not a rounding of a tie**: paired over the same eighteen seeds, `rank4` − `rank64` is **+0.0001331 ± 0.0000078 = 16.96σ, 18/18 positive** and `rank4` − `rank16` is **8.38σ**, so the number that should have been printed is resolved from the one that was, at 17σ. **What kept it invisible is the previous fire's subject**: the column holding the deltas was reported as *failing* for a reason that was false (no row matched the comparator), which masked its real state — **not checkable, and therefore unchecked** — and a checker that is wrong about *why* a thing fails is as bad as one that is wrong about *whether* it fails, because the first hides the second's absence. Corrected in place in §4.4 and marked. **The general form is rule 34's** — a sentence closed against a table by arithmetic, here between two artifacts — recorded as a candidate for `e132` rather than as a new tool. Cannot settle: **it does not make a `delta vs X` column checkable from inside its own table**, which remains true and is why the column is still reported as not checkable rather than as failing; **nothing in the table moves** (all four deltas, four σ's, four sign patterns, four LOO minima and four leverages match the stored fields at the printed precision, and *"the three worst candidates are the adaptive ones"* holds exactly, with the top three places occupied by `rank4`, `rank16` and `rank64`); and it is **one paragraph** — the rest of §4.4 and of the paper's number corpus is as the previous audits left it (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-worst-of-fourteen-was-a-different-candidate.md`) |
| `e123_loss_metric.py` over `--test 480` at read-out 128, 40 replicates, with `retention_loss` recorded (`e123`) | C2b | **pre-registered before its run**: the loss-valued retention matrix is continuous where the reported metric has granularity **1/240** — so does re-expressing the forgetting in nats remove the **thirty-fold** handicap `e118` measured against the drift? (`e123`) | **done — C0 is BIT-IDENTITY, the registered predictions FAIL, the falsifier FIRES, and the honest verdict is that the loss metric buys NOTHING — while the reason it looked worse turns out to be the units, and `e118`'s granularity diagnosis is measured and refuted as the explanation.** **C0 passes exactly**: the per-repeat accuracy forgetting equals `e119`'s forty replicates with **max ABS difference = 0.000e+00** and identical per-task rows, so recording `retention_loss` in the loop changed what is *recorded* and nothing about what is *trained*. **As registered**: P1 (`sd_loss/sd_acc < 1`) FAILS at **2.163**, P2 (`< 0.71`) FAILS, and the falsifier (`>= 1`) **FIRED** — the loss-valued matrix is 2.16x *noisier* than the metric it was meant to improve, and the falsifier's own stated consequence follows. **But that ratio divides 0.04790 nats by 0.02215 accuracy** — two different units — and **the registration contradicts itself in saying so**: P3 states that the claim *"is about the precision of each quantity against itself, never about agreement between them"*, while P1 and P2 are written as a cross-unit sd ratio. Read the way P3 says it must be: accuracy **0.04021 +/- 0.02215 → 55.1%**, loss **0.09561 +/- 0.04790 → 50.1%**, so **relative-sd(loss)/relative-sd(accuracy) = 0.910** — the loss is **9% better, i.e. the same thing**. Both readings agree on the verdict (*no improvement*, since 0.91 is nowhere near the registered 0.71), which is why the conclusion survives the flaw in the threshold, but **a cross-unit threshold that reaches the right verdict is still a threshold that should not have been registered**. **And the granularity floor is real, measured, and irrelevant** — the direct test of `e118`'s *mechanism* rather than its conclusion: the accuracy forgetting takes **30 distinct values of 40** replicates and the loss-valued one takes **40 of 40**, so the floor exists and the continuous quantity breaks it; removing it moves the relative spread by **9%**. **The two metrics rank the seeds almost identically** across the forty replicates (**r = +0.785**, Spearman **+0.800**, the log-ratio companion +0.695), so the finer scale carries no different signal. **Which refutes the granularity diagnosis as the explanation of the handicap**: the floor was there, was removed, and did nothing — so the eighteenfold gap against the drift is **intrinsic to the QUANTITY**, not to its estimator. "How much did this task's retained performance change" varies between seeds by about half its own value whether measured as an accuracy, an absolute loss, or a log ratio, and **no re-expression of a coarse thing is more precise than the thing it re-expresses** — which is a stronger form of §4.7's sentence than the project had. **Four corrections to my own analysis, each found by auditing it against the runner**: (1) the cross-unit threshold above; (2) **the wrong window** — the registration wrote the accuracy forgetting as `max_{k in [j,T-1]}`, and the runner computes `nanmax(R[:j+1, j])`, the window **0..j** (`experiments/e8_rate_network.py:517`); the implementation mirrored the mis-statement and the two windows differ by up to **0.0073 per replicate**, and fixing it moves the accuracy side from 53.5% to **55.1%** and the ratio from 0.937 to **0.910** — while leaving the **loss side identical under both windows**, because a task's loss is lowest at `k = j` and high at every earlier checkpoint, so the window cannot affect it; (3) **`sd/mean` on a log scale is an artifact** — the first version reported the log-ratio's precision as **12.6%**, which read as four times *better* than either metric and is meaningless, since adding 10 to every value divides it by five; the invariant form is multiplicative, **exp(sd) = 1.64x**, i.e. slightly *worse* than both linear forms, and the general lesson is that **a "relative" precision is only comparable on a linear scale**; (4) both sides of a paired comparison must be computed the same way, which (2) is an instance of and which is why the fire's numbers changed *after* the run — from a re-analysis of the stored artifact rather than a re-run. Cannot settle: the registration names read-out 128 **and 300**, and only 128 has run (the 300 arm's comparison is within that run, needing no comparator, but a ratio of 0.91 at one read-out is a point rather than a law); the loss is measured on the **training** split, whose 96 samples every seed interpolates (`e121`), so its sampling floor is not removable by a bigger test set and the ratio is a comparison of two precisions rather than the variance decomposition `e119` made; three tasks means the forgetting averages **two** numbers per replicate; and nothing here says the accuracy metric is the right one to report — only that its cheapest replacement is not. **AND THE REGISTERED SECOND READ-OUT REPLICATES IT**: read-out 300 (40 replicates, `--test 480`, C0 **bit-identical** to `runs/e119_r300_test480.json` with max ABS difference **0.000e+00**) gives accuracy **69.9%** and loss **63.5%** relative — worse than 128's 55/50% because read-out 300's forgetting is smaller (0.0269 against 0.0402) — with a **relative-sd ratio of 0.908 against read-out 128's 0.910, agreeing to 0.002**, and the falsifier firing again. So the stability is in **the ratio between the two estimators**, not in either one's own precision, and "about 9% better in relative precision" is now replicated rather than a point estimate (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-loss-metric-buys-nothing.md`) |
| the two-point comparison of `e116` (test 48) against `e123` (test 480), same 40 seeds (`e128`) | C2b | **analysis, no runs**: `evaluation_noise` reports a *nominal* removable share, and the two artifacts differ in exactly one config field — so what share is **achievable**? (`e128`) | **done — the nominal share is 85% and the achievable one is 29%, the paper's `2.6x` was stitched from a second artifact, and the explanation belonged to the other quantity.** **The pair is clean**: `runs/e116_r128_40reps.json` and `runs/e123_r128_test480.json` differ in **exactly one config field**, `test` (48 against 480), with the same 40 seeds — verified field by field. The nominal binomial sem falls **2.98×** against the 3.16 a genuinely independent tenfold set gives, so **the formula is internally consistent**, while the accuracy's per-replicate sd falls only **1.163×**; a 85.3% removable share with a tenfold test set predicts **2.07×**. **Solving the two equations for the one piece the pair can identify** — taking the training component as constant — gives an effective binomial sem of **0.01229 against a nominal 0.02109** (1.72× smaller), i.e. **an effective 49 independent held-out decisions rather than 144, and an achievable removable share of 29% rather than 85%**. **The runner's own else-branch had carried that caveat as a clause since it was written** — *"a fraction above 100% is a signal, not a number … or the 144 held-out decisions are not independent"* — and this is the first time it is measured rather than invoked. **And the section's explanation belongs to the other quantity**: it attributed the over-prediction to the *difference* of two accuracies, but the forgetting's removable component **does** fall by √10 — *because the 60% split was solved for by assuming it, which makes that a tautology rather than a test* — while the **single accuracy's** directly-computed 85% is the one that fails, predicting 2.07× against a measured **1.16×**, so **the correlation between two accuracies is not the mechanism and the mechanism is upstream of it, in the held-out decisions themselves**. **And the paper's `2.6x` never followed from its own `85%`**: 2.6× needs a **94.7%** share, and 0.9467 is `runs/e102_rate_fb8_omp1.json`'s **0.9461** — an artifact at **read-out 32 with five replicates** against the **read-out 128 / forty** the 85% comes from, so **a sentence paired a statistic from one run with a prediction computed from another's**. That is rule 28's *"a column whose comparator is not one number"* **in prose rather than in a table**, which is why `e97` (artifact existence), `e105` (table closure) and `e126` (counts) all passed it and why it survived from `e119` to this fire: **both halves are real numbers from real artifacts and neither is wrong on its own**. **The check is the cheapest kind and needs nothing on disk — recompute the stated prediction from the stated input**, one line for any `f`; the corrected arithmetic is **2.07×**, and the honest sentence is one line shorter, since the forgetting's 1.47× is definitional and there is no contrast between the two to report. Repaired in the paper and dated in the source finding. **Which strengthens rather than weakens the section's conclusion**: if only 29% of the accuracy's variance is removable at all then `e119`'s tenfold test set captured **even more** of what was available than its 94% said, the test-set route is **more exhausted** than it looked, and the residual is even more clearly the substrate's. **AND IT REPLICATES AT A SECOND READ-OUT, WHERE THE NOMINAL MODEL GIVES UP ENTIRELY**: the pair `runs/e115_r300_40reps.json` (test 48) against `runs/e123_r300_test480.json` gives an effective binomial sem **2.07×** smaller than nominal, an effective **n_eval of 33.5 against a nominal 144**, and an **achievable removable share of 26%** — and there the nominal model is not merely optimistic but **incoherent**, because `variance_fraction = 1.1165`, i.e. the binomial sem it computes (0.01983) **exceeds the observed per-replicate sd** (0.01877), which is exactly the case the runner's own else-branch prints as *"this run cannot separate them"* rather than as a number, and why the sd fall it predicts is **undefined rather than a number**. **The two-point solve is well-posed anyway** because it never uses the nominal sem as an input — only the two observed sds and the one fact the design licenses, that the sampling component falls by √10. So **the nominal block fails in two different ways at two read-outs, optimistic at 128 and incoherent at 300**, and the removable share is **unknown from the block alone and measurable from a pair** (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-nominal-removable-share-is-not-the-achievable-one.md`) |
| `e127_programme_table_audit.py` over the plan's programme table (`e127`) | C2b | **analysis, and it makes rule 22's check executable** — the check that found eight stale rows in `e85` and three self-contradicting ones in a later pass was run **by hand both times** and was never code, which is the condition that produced four drifts in the same direction (`e127`) | **done — the table passes all four mechanical checks, and the first automated pass found a broken row in the very table it audits, added to that table earlier in the same session.** **86 rows parsed, 78 carrying a status indicator**, and four checks, one per documented failure mode: **(A)** every `runs/*.json` the row names exists — **0** flags; **(B)** no status cell announces itself as open and then reports an answer — **0**; **(C)** no two rows share a first cell — **0**; **(D)** no row contains a raw pipe in a cell — **0, after one repair**. **The repair is the finding**: the `e124` row added to this table earlier in this same session contained an absolute-value expression written with two BARE PIPE CHARACTERS inside a code span, and in a GFM table a literal pipe separates cells **unless each pipe is escaped with a backslash, and that holds even inside backticks** — so the row rendered as **two** rows and broke the table between them for every reader. **None of this project's audits could have caught it**: `e97` reads artifact existence, `e105` reads a table's arithmetic, `e126` reads counts, and the numbers here were all correct — a broken row is a claim about the *table* rather than about the values in it. **And it was introduced by the same session that then audited it**, which is the ordinary case rather than a coincidence: the table is edited by whoever is working, and nobody re-reads a rendered version to check a row is still one row. **Three false-positive classes, all of which would have made the checker worse than nothing**, per rule 22's own last paragraph: (1) **it split rows on every pipe**, so a correctly-escaped absolute-value expression was reported malformed — one false alarm and no catch on its first run — and the split now respects the backslash escape while the reported context centres on the first pipe *beyond* the row's five structural borders (centring on the fifth showed the row's own trailing border and nothing else); (2) **it flagged "an open word after a done marker" as a contradiction, and fired on 9 of 9 rows as false positives** — every one a status cell reading `done — …` with the word "launched" or "PENDING" in later **prose about a different experiment**, including the row whose own correction note quotes the phrase it was being flagged for; this is the failure rule 22 already had on record from its manual passes, reproduced exactly by the automated one, so it is now **counted as a denominator and never as a finding**; (3) **artifacts named in the `why` column were never checked** — the search covered `what` and `status` only, and `why` is where a row says what closed a gap, so a row citing its gap's artifact there would have had check A report **zero while skipping it**, which is the failure mode where a checker's silence looks like coverage; a test caught it. **Two more came from the tests rather than the corpus**: the column header and the all-dashes separator row were both being counted as data rows (the separator's own cells contain pipe characters, so the skip condition never fired), and a test that hard-coded a line number had assumed the wrong table offset. Cannot settle: **check B is a word test and rule 22's fourth failure is not a word** — four rows marked done while summarising a number the project had since overturned, where the status word is right and the *number* is stale, needs the result re-derived from an artifact and **nothing does that for this table's prose**; the self-contradiction rule has a **false-negative side**, since *"print the opening of each status cell beside the end of it and ask whether they agree"* is a **reading** instruction and a machine can only compare keywords, so a cell that opens `done —` and closes with a retraction of its own opening passes; **C is a text match** on the first cell, so two rows describing one piece of work in different words are not caught; and the four checks are the four documented modes, so this is a claim that the table is **self-consistent**, never that it is right (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-rule-22-had-no-code.md`) |
| `e126_enumeration_audit.py` over the paper, the plan and the whole findings corpus (`e126`) | C2b | **analysis, the first check here that reads a document's own claims about its own shape** — every other audit reads numbers (`e97` artifact existence, `e105` table closure, `e103` arm re-execution) (`e126`) | **done — two defects had been in the paper for two days, and a third was found on the way; the corpus is now clean at this audit's precision.** **(1)** §7's trap list heading said **"Five measurement traps" over four bullets**, and `git log -S` dates it exactly: commit `1743573` rewrote *"Two measurement traps"* to **"Five"** while adding two bullets to the two that were there, so it said five and listed four from the day it was written — **and that commit's message is about stale aggregate statements**, so the defect it was fixing was committed in the fix; repaired to **six** with the two traps this session found (a saved state can omit a parameter and the omission is invisible until something is recomputed from it; a run compared with a copy of *itself* is still a contrast across code epochs). **(2) And the mirror image, which is worse because it is a self-contradiction rather than a stale number**: §4.3 said at line 666 *"All eight ladder rungs now have a measured draw sd of their own"* and fourteen lines later at 681 that the column *"is a floor, since three of its eight rungs have no measurement of their own"*. `runs/e73_ladder_named_head_to_head.json` decides it in one field — **`n_ladder_measured` = 8 of `n_ladder` = 8**, every one of the eight rows carrying `draw_sd_measured: true` — so the abstract and 666 are right and 681 was stale, written when five of eight were measured and missed by **two** later commits that corrected its sibling paragraph and its abstract twin. **The tell that needed no tooling: the stale clause quoted the post-`e74` range (2.6–13.7σ) while giving the pre-`e74` reason**, a sentence internally inconsistent with itself. Repaired to name `e74` (the last three) and `e90` (the pair that had been *borrowed*, since `pool32` and `pool64` are the same partition at d = 1307) — a detail the abstract's "all eight" also glosses. **(3)** §4.3's σ table printed `pool4` at **42.2σ** with no marker while the abstract warns it "overstates it several-fold"; it now says in place that the column is against zero with a seed-only sem and an assumed 1.0e-3 draw sd, that the corrected column is **2.6–13.7σ** (a **3.1×** overstatement at the best rung) and that the head-to-head moves from an order of magnitude to a factor of **1.9** — §9's own rule that naming the command behind a superseded number is worse than naming none. **Checked and correct, which is most of it**: the abstract's **"Thirteen"** retractions (12 top-level entries plus one nested in a parenthesis, and the sentence's own "the thirteenth is the one whose number survived" matches the last entry); **§4.2.1's "four attempts"** against its four bullets; and **§4.2.1's "six further experiments"** against nine cited ids — left alone rather than rewritten, because all nine ids are real and the counts reconcile, but the record does not let it be *verified* and rewriting an unsourceable count would be worse than a loose one. **The audit's four failed designs are recorded in its docstring**, because each is a way a checker is worse than nothing: matching any number-adjacent-to-a-list gave **~40 mismatches of which not one was real**; counting a numbered list like a bulleted one read the abstract's four findings as three (the mismatch was *manufactured*, and the repair is that for `1. 2. 3.` the numbering is the document's own length claim, so a block not starting at 1 is a mid-list fragment and is skipped); widening the lookback let the §7 correction note's own phrase *"adding two bullets"* claim 2 over a six-item list; and the residual two false alarms came from crossing a section heading. **Two more bugs were caught only by the test suite** — `CLAIM_RE` had been tightened until it could no longer match *"Five measurement traps"*, so the checker had stopped catching the defect it exists for while still reporting a clean corpus, and Markdown's lazy-continuation rule was missing so the paper's item 3, whose wrapped tail lost its indent, read as three items. Result: **166 documents, 9 count claims found next to a list, 9 matched, 0 mismatches, 0 numbering holes, 205 ratio phrases reported as not-checked**, with the denominator asserted in the test suite (`found >= 5`) because a checker that finds nothing passes every silence test. Cannot settle: it reads **one** pattern, so a stale count buried mid-sentence — `e125`'s cited experiment list, §4.2.1's "six further experiments", `e97`'s own denominators, which is most counts in this corpus — is invisible to it; the 17-noun lexicon is a judgement and a defect whose noun is missing will be missed **silently rather than reported as unchecked**; it cannot distinguish "Two things worth noting" over four bullets (not an error) from "Five measurement traps" over four (an error), which is why it needs the opening rule and the heading rule and will still be wrong somewhere; and it only says a document **agrees with itself**, never that the stated count is the right one (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-paper-counted-its-own-bullets-wrong.md`) |
| `e8_rate_network.py` --frozen-body at read-out 0 / 128 / 32, and `--methods naive,ewc` at the two upper settings (`e104`) | C2b | **the cheapest repair the control audit identified**: fill the cells that match no artifact, rather than arguing about them (`e104`) | **done — and the unbacked claim was largely RIGHT, which the record could not have told anyone.** Read-out **0 / 128 / 32**: plastic acc 0.9333 / 0.9333 / 0.9139 with forgetting **+0.0479 / +0.0333 / +0.0729**; frozen acc **0.9444 / 0.9167 / 0.8139** with forgetting **+0.0000 ± 0.0000 at all three** (fifteen replicates agreeing to the last digit — structural, not small). then 25 checks / 5 mismatches; then 27 / 4, all four from the single row that *quotes* the three fields in which its derived command differs; then **24 / 0** after (a) a label with **zero** files is not a reference (it is how this table names a runner: `e3`, `e8`) while a label with **several** files is one (`e140` is four artifacts), (b) a clause introduced by *against* or *vs* names a **baseline** rather than the row's own output, (c) the command **ends where the cell first names its artifact** — the table's own registering convention, "its artifact to be written as `X` under `runs/`" — and (d) a list-valued config agrees with a member of it (`--topologies real` runs `["real"]`). **The licensed sentence is narrow, and the ledger is why**: only 6 of 138 rows can be attributed at all — 82 state no flag, 43 state flags but name no artifact they resolve (they name their **runner**, and their artifact is in the status column), 3 name more than one artifact, 1 names a label that identifies several files, 1 names an artifact only as a comparison partner, and 2 artifacts carry no `config`; Falsifier: any artifact whose `readout` block disagrees with the rule — the test suite writes such an artifact to prove the check fires |

A checker that only asks whether a row announces itself as open *after* reporting an answer cannot see a row that announces itself as open and never updates — the same error with the answer written in another file | **done — 4 rows were under-claiming the disk, every one of them already read somewhere else, and all four are fixed.** `e92` says *"launched, prediction before the run"* with **61 artifacts** in `runs/` including `runs/e92_grid_report.json`, read by two findings a day earlier; `e147` says *"the `3e-3` arm is running"* with `runs/e147_r32_frozenbias_ewc_lam3e-3.json` on disk, and that arm decided the registered **falsifier** (the λ step under the freeze is -0.0047 ± 0.0032 = 1.46σ, below its 2σ bar); `e141` says *"the top of the sweep is running"* with `runs/e141_r32_ewc_lam3e-{1,2}.json` on disk; and `e6` says *"relaunched after a caught failure"* with `runs/e64_predictor_6_perseed.json` on disk. C now reads **0 of 111 rows** — the 111 being the denominator, since only rows that name their own experiment in one of the two recognised ways can be checked at all (`docs/findings/2026-09-25-the-check-the-table-promised-and-never-ran.md`) |

refusal. **Limits**: the axis has only two points (0.0 and 1.0), so this is a direction and not a dose–response — an intermediate `--input-overlap` is a command rather than a code change and is the The mechanism the split suggests, as a hypothesis: sharing an input population shares a **measurement** in the analytic line (so a posterior update from one task is not harmful to the other) and puts two tasks' templates on the same neurons behind one **decoder** in the network line (so the directions compete) — testable with per-task heads, which 17 corpus runs already use. Two limits are stated rather than implied — `present` is necessary and not sufficient, because findings quote superseded numbers in order to correct them, and the witness column is the first match in a fixed file order, so a common number such as 4.7 has several. 10% of the paper's quoted numbers are therefore supported but not localised by the citation beside them, and that is a documentation fact with a measured size (`docs/findings/2026-09-25-the-papers-numbers-are-all-supported-and-a-tenth-are-not-localised.md`) |

The difference is structural rather than a defect: a plan row summarises a whole experiment, so its citation anchors the row while its numbers come from wherever they were measured. **And the two exceptions were read rather than reported**: `1.5e-9` is an agreement between two `replay` arms computed IN THE ROW, and `40.5` is a heartbeat's projection from `e193`'s launch — both are the document's own arithmetic, which is the class this run adds to the audit's record and the reason its exit code is NOT a gate on the plan (the paper's remains the gate that means 'nothing the document claims is unsupported'). elapsed 0.3 min, projected 40.5 min, cpu/wall 17.6x` through `[8/120] … cp/wall 18.2x`, so the run's own progress, its projection from replicates completed and its effective parallelism are all readable while it works — which `e178` could not do. **The first price refinement rule 49 owes**: the registered 5.8–9.6 h was **360 arm-replicates at an average** of 57–96 s taken from whole commands; the `naive` arm alone runs at **20–22 s per arm-replicate**, and the block arms are what make the average, so the average cannot be extrapolated to a single arm and the per-arm rates are to be read off the heartbeat as each arm starts. **Measured, first heartbeats of each arm**: `naive` **20–25 s** per arm-replicate (its 40 replicates in **16.4 min**, projecting 49.1 min for the level's 120), then `ewc-block` **67 s** for its first replicate — a **2.7–3.3×** spread between two arms of the same command, so the whole-level projection is only meaningful once the expensive arms are running, and the registered 5.8–9.6 h is a whole-command average that no single arm obeys. **And the pairing was certified BEFORE the artifacts exist**, which is what declaring the inert fields is for: the launched commands differ from `e140` (the disjoint baseline the dose read pairs against) in **`methods` alone** — `input_overlap` is the manipulation and is excluded by construction — and `methods` is inert for `naive`, `ewc-block` and `ewc-block-rand`, so all three levels will be admitted; a test now asserts that, so an edit to the design fails before a multi-hour run rather than the read refusing the pair afterwards. The read is `e191 --dose runs/e193_r32_overlap{n}_methods_40reps.json` (added while this ran), which pairs each level against the disjoint baseline arm by arm and checks P1's bar — half the measured 0→1 rise of +0.1234, i.e. +0.0617 — per level. The x-axis is measured first and is commensurable with the analytic line's: the same `overlap_controlled_supports` construction at seed 0 on `mb+cx+al@n1307` achieves Jaccard **0.0000 / 0.1429 / 0.3333 / 0.6000 / 1.0000** for targets 0.00 / 0.25 / 0.50 / 0.75 / 1.00 — the same four decimals `e7`'s controlled sweep reports, so the shape comparison needs no conversion. **P1 — monotone and more than half done by the midpoint**: the adjacent-pair interference at achieved 0.3333 lies between its 0.0 and 1.0 values and closer to the 1.0 end (for `naive`, between +0.1330 and +0.2564 and above +0.1947). **P2 — the shape matches the analytic timing**: the analytic near component is 80% of the way through its fall by achieved 0.3333, so the network's near rise should be ≥60% complete there if the two lines share a mechanism with opposite sign. **Falsifier — the axis is a step**: the near component at achieved 0.1429 equals its 1.0 value within 2σ. **Null worth keeping**: all three intermediates ~1σ, which leaves the direction on the two-point contrast while the shape stays unmeasured. **Cost from the artifacts' own `timing_s`** (rule 49): 360 arm-replicates at **57.0 s** (`e133`, 2 methods) to **96.1 s** (`e140`, 5 methods) each = **5.8–9.6 h**, the machine's load having varied 67% across the five artifacts measured. The read is `e191` and `e188`, which take artifact paths (`docs/findings/2026-09-25-registered-the-overlap-axis-at-three-intermediate-levels.md`) |
| `e185`'s baseline arm made evidence-based, and the two collector defects it exposed (`e185`) | C2b | **the check that verifies the benchmark block's baseline list was matching three WORDS rather than files, and its list collector was reading nothing** — so a phantom baseline sat inside a clean zero | **done — the block claimed a joint-training upper bound that exists nowhere, and `e185` had blessed it.** The corpus's `methods` are exactly `naive` (129 artifacts), `ewc` (49), `ewc-block` (38), `ewc-block-rand` (41) and `replay` (34); `grep joint` over `clfly/` and `experiments/` finds one local variable in `e36` and the checker's own word list. The three control words (`oracle`, `frozen`, `joint`) are replaced by an evidence map: a control counts when a **flag** exists in some parser (through `e172`'s registry, so `frozen` is evidenced by `--frozen-body` and `replay` by `--replay-batch`) or a **module** carries its name (`oracle` by `clfly/bench/oracle.py`), and `joint` resolves to neither, so it is now offered as proposed and named in the prose rather than in the list. **And two collector defects came out with it**: `marked()` broke out of the loop at the first prose line, so every mark after a paragraph was invisible and the baseline and proposed arms reported clean zeros while reading **nothing** — a synthetic block with no prose between its lists could not see it, which is why the bug survived its own tests — and a wrapped bullet ended its list, so only the first item of the proposed list was ever read (now 12 items, and 3 in the implemented list and 6 in the baseline one). A path inside a bullet is treated as the bullet's *evidence* rather than another claim, which was the first new flag after the list came back to life. The live gate now asserts the baseline list is **non-empty** before believing its verdict, which is the guard against exactly this class of vacuity (`docs/findings/2026-09-25-the-baseline-list-had-three-words-and-two-of-them-were-real.md`) |
| `e190_side_rung_read.py` — the read `e178` was launched for, written before its artifact exists (`e190`) | C2b | **a reader written ahead of the data, so that the verdict is one command and not a fresh argument about which quantity the claim is about** — the registration's arithmetic is on per-replicate ACCURACY and the same three replicates give the opposite sign pattern on forgetting | **done as an instrument, verified against the one artifact that exists.** The reader reproduces `runs/e10_rung_side.json`'s registration numbers exactly: three replicates at `+0.0278`, `-0.0625`, `-0.0000`, **mean -0.0116, sd 0.0462, 0.43σ from zero**, 2 of 3 negative — and prints the same three replicates' **forgetting** pairing as `-0.0521`, `+0.0833`, `-0.0208` (mean +0.0035), which is the opposite sign pattern and the reason the reader prints both and names which is registered. Against the missing `e178_rung_side_cs300_144reps.json` (no `runs/` prefix: it has not been written) it prints the four registered outcomes and claims nothing ("no number is printed from a design whose arms do not exist"); against a real payload it prints the paired gap with n, mean, sd, sem, σ and the negative/positive split, the comparator's own numbers for P2, and the recorded cost (**`e10`'s own `timing_s` is 6328 s = 1.76 h**, rule 49's real-duration requirement). The verdict for the cs = 800 triple comes out **UNRESOLVED AT 3 REPLICATES (the null worth keeping)**, P1 not holding and the falsifier not firing — which is what the registration says about the triple (`docs/findings/2026-09-25-the-side-rungs-negative-priced-before-it-is-bought.md` section 3) |

Every figure carries its control arm, and every recall/precision number in this document
carries a resolvability check. The prediction scoreboard, including the refutations,
lives in `docs/findings/`.

## Method

Research here runs as a bounded, measured loop: one metric, constrained scope,
automatic rollback, git as the record. Phase 1's gate was `repro_max_abs_err`,
now closed at 4.7e-5.

Two rules that keep the loop honest:

1. **The oracle stays in.** Every experiment keeps the Kalman/RTS reference line,
   so "better than the baseline" can always be read as a fraction of the gap that
   actually exists.
2. **Failures ship.** The Phase-1 negative result (no unimodal peak in the
   coordinate basis) is in the findings log with the same prominence as the
   successes, because it is what redirected the programme.

### Measurement rules

Added 2026-09-22, after the headline metric was found to be chaotic
(`docs/findings/2026-09-22-metric-instability.md`).

3. **Do not report the relative gap bare.** On the connectome substrate EWC's
   relative excess over the oracle has a standard deviation comparable to its own
   mean — up to 1.38 across seeds at *fixed* settings — and moves by ±0.04 under a
   **1e-15 relative** change in the spectral radius. It is a ratio of two small,
   close numbers, so it inherits the EWC estimator's full relative variance.
   Report `clfly.bench.oracle.paired_excess`: the **absolute** excess with its
   standard error across >= 3 seeds, plus `gap_of_means` if a ratio is wanted.
   At current settings, excess differences below ~0.05 are not resolvable.

   > **Measured at n = 12 (`e55`), and corrected in two ways.**
   >
   > **The stated reason is not diagnostic.** The absolute excess's across-seed CV is
   > **0.70–1.26** at the seven κ values, against the relative gap's **0.73–1.15** — the same.
   > What the absolute metric fixes is the *ratio-of-two-close-numbers* pathology (the 1-ULP
   > sensitivity and the 1.38 CV), **not the across-seed spread**. A reader who takes this rule as
   > "the absolute excess is more stable across seeds" is wrong by a factor of one.
   >
   > **The 0.05 is stale and is the wrong kind of number.** The across-seed sd of the absolute excess
   > has a median over κ of **0.01971**, so the unpaired minimum detectable difference (2.8 × sem,
   > 80% power) is **0.0159 at n = 12** and **0.0319 at n = 3** — 3.1× and 1.6× below the quoted 0.05.
   > **But the project's contrasts are within-seed differences**, where the same measurement on `e48`
   > gives **0.00100** — and *not* because pairing cancels much (the arms correlate at only r = +0.29),
   > but because the *difference* is stable relative to its signal while either arm is comparable to
   > its own mean. So this rule needs a **pair** of numbers with the comparison named: the basis deltas
   > the neuron ladder resolves (0.0015–0.0040) look unmeasurable against 0.0159 and are comfortably
   > resolved against 0.0010.
   >
   > **And attach the configuration.** `excess(real)` at cs = 800 has an across-seed sd of 0.01971 in
   > `e5`'s uniform-drive sweep and **0.00175** in `e2`'s default-drive run — the same topology, circuit
   > and seed count, differing in the task construction. "At current settings" covers an 11× range.
   > (`docs/findings/2026-09-22-the-resolution-figure-is-stale-and-wrong-shaped.md`)
4. **Pin the spectral radius, and report it.** `stable_weights` must use a
   deterministic ARPACK start vector. With the default random start, `W` differs by
   1 ULP between identical calls and the reported gap moves by several percent.
   This was a real bug, found and closed.
5. **Geometry is safe; realized errors are not.** Quantities computed from
   eigenvectors (task overlap, effective rank) are bit-reproducible. Anything built
   from a realized estimation error is not. Prefer geometric statements.
6. **Watch for range-space degeneracy.** Taking the top-`rank` subspace of a
   rank-`rank` matrix returns its entire range space, which for these tasks is
   determined by support *membership* and is blind to how strongly each neuron is
   driven. A predictor built on it cannot see spectral structure — the most likely
   reason the principal-angle alignment scalar failed in `e3`. Use
   `task_subspaces(..., top=k)` for a spectrally selected subspace.
7. **"Resolves from zero" is not "differs from its neighbour".** A claim about the
   *shape* of a curve — where an optimum sits, whether an advantage is monotone —
   needs contrasts between the curve's own points, not a per-point σ against zero.
   Contrasts must be **paired on the seed**, since every base in a run sees the same
   task geometries in the same order: use `clfly.bench.analytic.paired_delta` and
   `contrast_of_contrasts`, which report the seed correlation ρ and both sems. The
   quadrature figure is not a bound — a matched difference has variance
   ``sd_a^2 + sd_b^2 − 2ρ sd_a sd_b``, so it is the *larger* one when ρ < 0. And
   order the points by granularity before contrasting them; an alphabetically sorted
   table makes "adjacent" meaningless.
8. **Store per-seed values.** A pooled mean and sem cannot be re-analysed paired,
   and re-deriving a two-hour run to recover them is avoidable: `analytic_excess`
   records `excess_per_seed`. `--report-from <json>` re-prints any finished run's
   report without recomputing it.
9. **Time the pieces before calling a configuration untestable.** Twice now a
   scientific conclusion rested on an implementation artefact — a benchmark that
   measured its decoder, and a "coarse rungs are too slow" reading that was 4×
   redundant work inside the step loop (a constant Fisher re-converted from numpy
   on every step; `SynapsePartition.make_penalty` binds it once per task instead).
   When something is too slow to run, profile it before concluding the science is
   expensive. Related: a module with no tests hid a dtype crash one argument away
   — the penalty was one float64 anchor from raising.
10. **A matched-random control must be averaged over draws.** The control is the
   *population* of size-matched random partitions, and one draw is one sample from
   it. The draw-to-draw sd is ordered by the partition's **concentration**,
   ``sum_g s_g^2 / d^2`` (`clfly.bench.control.concentration`), **not by its group
   count**. It is a **coarse/fine** axis only: on d = 1307 the coarse range
   0.325–0.678 measures 6.1e-4 to 1.06e-3 draw sd with no ordering — 1.01e-3 at
   0.395 against 6.1e-4 at 0.459, a non-monotone 40% swing between two runs of the
   same experiment — and the level differs between circuits (4.9e-4 at d = 952
   against 1.06e-3 at d = 1307, at the same concentration near 0.7). A group
   count gets `side` exactly backwards — 4 groups but a concentration of 0.498,
   so it belongs with the coarse partitions. Concentration 1.0 means a single
   group, i.e. the `Full` basis with excess exactly zero — at d = 952 two ladder
   rungs are that, so they are not granularity points at all. **For a budget,
   use ≈1e-3 for any coarse rung rather than an interpolation**: it is the top of
   the observed range, so it overstates the required K rather than understating
   it. The interpolation is nevertheless usable when a measurement is unavailable,
   but its error is not bounded: four columns measured so far give ratios of
   1.15×, 1.18×, 1.26× and **2.0×** (`supertype`). Where the draw term dominates —
   the coarse partitions — the error has been 15–26%; the 2× case is a fine column
   whose seed sem is an order of magnitude larger than the draw sd, so it does not
   matter there. The two failure modes are disjoint, which is why a constant budget
   and a seed-dominated fine column are both safe, and neither should be assumed of
   the other. Where the draw term
   dominates, a single-draw σ overstates the evidence several-fold: coarse-rung σ
   in the basis study are provisional, `pool4` 42.2σ → ≈8.7σ with the component
   included, and `pool2 → pool4` (the plateau question) 2.2σ → ≈0.6σ. Report the
   two components separately, and never rest a claim about a curve's *shape* on
   single-draw controls. The paired column needs ≥12 seeds to be usable: at 3
   seeds the sample correlation hits ±1.00 by chance and σ_p disagrees with σ by
   up to 3×. **And this rule was violated for the whole network record until 2026-09-24**:
   `e8_rate_network.py` built one `random_matched` partition **outside** the replicate
   loop, so all forty replicates of an `ewc-block-rand` arm shared a **single** draw and that
   arm's sem contained **no draw term at all** — while the linear line had `--control-draws`
   and recorded `sd_across_draws` the whole time. `--partition-seed` now names the draw
   (defaulting to `--seed0`, so every artifact written before it is bit-identical under an
   unchanged command) and the artifact records a **fingerprint** of the partition, so two
   draws can be told apart without storing the partition. **The honest form of every
   `ewc-block-rand` number quoted before that date — `e135`'s −0.0125, the paper's §4.2 block
   rows — is *"one draw"* rather than *"the control"***, and the network's own draw sd is
   **measured the same day, on two families**: two draws of one arm on the same forty seeds differ by
   **1.33σ (0.0138)** on the shared-input family and by **2.33σ (0.01875)** on the base family — against the
   ≈1e-3 above, which is a measurement of the **linear** substrate's excess and not of this network's
   forgetting. **And the consequence is on record**: the base family's central contrast was **2.08σ** in favour
   of the biology against one draw and **0.26σ** against the second, with the mean of the two at 1.49σ — so the
   strongest single-draw value this project has ever measured for its own central contrast **did not survive one
   more sample of its control**, and the paper's claim was strengthened rather than threatened by the
   measurement that was expected to break it. The three-draw machinery is `--partition-seed` and the
   `partition_draw` fingerprint, and the amendment that reads a contrast against the **mean of K draws** with
   the between-draw component reported beside the paired one
   (`docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-draw-moves-the-control-by-0014.md`,
   `docs/findings/2026-09-24-the-base-familys-advantage-does-not-survive-a-second-draw.md`).
11. **Artifacts are strict JSON.** Non-finite floats go in as `null`, never as `NaN`
   or `Infinity` — Python reads those back happily, and `JSON.parse`, `serde_json`,
   `encoding/json` and `pandas.read_json` all refuse them. Seven rate-network
   artifacts were unreadable to a strict consumer before this was noticed. Write
   through `clfly.bench.artifacts.write_json`; do not call `json.dump` directly.
12. **Budget a question before buying it.** The cost of settling a curve claim is
   set by how far the effect sits above its floor, and the floor may not involve
   the parameter you were about to spend compute measuring. Working that out
